## 2026-07-28 Juno Array Notification Policy

- Per-task `EXIT` notifications suppress routine success but continue sending
  failures and other abnormal outcomes.
- Live parent-array summaries query the expanded user queue and filter by
  `ArrayJobID`, so they include both pending and running counts, for example
  `5 PENDING, 4 RUNNING`.
- The parent `scripts/job_notifier.py slurm <array-jobid>` watcher must always
  send the final aggregate terminal summary, including all-success messages
  such as `✅ Array 307993 finished: 16 COMPLETED`.
- Regression coverage is in `utils/tests/test_job_notifications.py`.
- Array `309094` completed `15/15`; its watcher delivered the final aggregate
  completion notification.

  - launchers:

    - corrected mix-scaled sweep:

  - common setup:
    - dataset `celeba`
    - model `resnet`
    - attributes `redhair_brownskin_sademotion`
    - ratio `0.1`
    - generated images `1000`
    - base checkpoint `save/weights/model_celeba_resnet_base.pth`
    - focus attributes `hair color,skin,emotion`
    - entropy `False`
    - explicit generated-count injection:
      - `hair color:red=1000,skin:brown=1000,emotion:sad=1000`
    - corrected mix-scaled follow-up:
      - `al_attribute_extra_count_scale = 10`
      - effective injected counts during scoring:
        - `hair color:red=10000`
        - `skin:brown=10000`
        - `emotion:sad=10000`

- Why the mix-scaled sweep supersedes the earlier raw-count probe:
  - the selector scores against the full original candidate pool, not the final selected subset
  - on CelebA `ratio=0.1`, raw `1000` extra counts were being diluted against `80,000` originals during scoring
  - but the final training mix is only `8000 original + 1000 generated`
  - scaling generated counts by `1 / sub_ratio = 10` makes the selector see a generated-image share much closer to the final training regime

- Why explicit `extra_counts` are required on CelebA:
  - `save/attribute.json` uses spaced metadata keys such as:
    - `hair color`
    - `skin`
    - `emotion`
  - the helper that auto-infers generated counts from `redhair_brownskin_sademotion` does not map those spaced keys correctly
  - without the explicit `extra_counts` flag, the frequency-penalty term would ignore the synthetic flood and the CelebA attribute sweep would not match the intended ImageNet-style setup

- Final mix-scaled 8-run results:
  - `mixselx10_e0_r003_p05_cinf`
    - held-out test accuracy `90.99%`
    - AL selection time `0.74 s`

- Result ranking and takeaway:
  - best config:
    - `mixselx10_e0_r003_p05_cinf`
    - `R=0.03`, `P=0.5`, `C=inf`, `E=False`, `extra_count_scale=10`
  - improvement over the earlier raw-count best:
    - `90.99%` vs `90.59%`
    - `+0.40 pp`
  - but the corrected attribute path still does not beat the completed CelebA `ratio=0.1` baselines:
    - `random`: `91.15%`
    - `badge`: `91.12%`
    - `stratified`: `90.95%`
  - current gap to `random`:
    - `-0.16 pp`
  - conclusion:
    - correcting the generated-count scale materially improves CelebA attribute AL, but it is still slightly below the best completed baseline at `ratio=0.1`

- The immediate problem is algorithmic scalability in the Active Learning selectors, not GPU allocation:
  - `CoreSetStrategy.greedy_k_center()` in `utils/active_learning.py` does a full distance update for every newly selected point
  - `BadgeStrategy._kmeanspp_seeding()` uses the same repeated full-distance pattern
  - `HierarchicalActiveStrategy.select()` in `utils/hierarchical_al.py` repeatedly scans all samples for slice membership and then runs greedy distance-based within-slice selection
  - on CelebA with `80000` original samples, these selectors become impractically slow at larger `sub_ratio` values

- Strong evidence from logs:
  - `badge` ratio `0.1` did finish but already needed:
    - `Total Active Learning selection time: 6695.79 seconds`
    - log:
      - `logs/celeba/resume_lr1e-5_epoch15_badge_resnet_celeba_1000_ratio0.1.log`
  - `badge` ratios `0.3` and `0.5`, and `hierarchical` ratio `0.1`, never advanced beyond the initial three startup lines in their worker logs
  - `coreset` ratio `0.7` was still progressing in:
    - `logs/celeba/resume_lr1e-5_epoch15_coreset_resnet_celeba_1000_ratio0.7.log`
    - but only reached progress `47600/56000` before being stopped

    - `./scripts/run_celeba_resume_baselines.sh all`
  - supported strategies:

- BADGE implementation details in this repo:
  - uses the same pretrained embedding extraction already used by `coreset` / `diversity`
  - builds a lightweight proxy classifier from validation-set class centroids in embedding space
  - forms BADGE-style gradient embeddings from those proxy probabilities
  - uses k-means++ seeding to pick the final batch

- `pose_estimation/train_pose_estimation.py` checkpoint saving was changed on April 15, 2026 to reduce disk usage while preserving post-train analysis:
  - new default behavior:
    - `best.pth` is now a compact model-only checkpoint
    - `last.pth` is now optional and is **not** saved by default
    - the default saved checkpoint contains:
      - `model_state_dict`
      - `epoch`
      - `best_ap`
  - optional compact last-model artifact:
    - pass `--save-last-checkpoint true`
    - this writes `last.pth` as another compact model-only checkpoint
  - optional full resume artifact:
    - pass `--save-resume-checkpoint true`
    - this additionally writes `last_resume.pth` with:
      - model
      - optimizer
      - lr_scheduler
      - epoch
      - best_ap
- Rationale:
  - the training loop's post-train val/test evaluation and slice analysis only need model weights
  - they do not need optimizer or scheduler state
  - therefore the default `best.pth` / `last.pth` no longer need to store full training state
- Size validation:
  - a temporary model-only checkpoint written from an existing Qwen3 pose checkpoint was `226.1 MB`
  - the existing full checkpoints are about `451 MB`
  - therefore the new default saves about `225 MB` per checkpoint
  - because `last.pth` is now off by default, the new default normally saves only one compact checkpoint (`best.pth`)
  - compared with the old default `best.pth + last.pth` full-checkpoint pair (about `902 MB` total), the new default usually saves about `676 MB` per run
- Resume semantics after this change:
  - `--resume <ckpt>` now expects a full training-state checkpoint and will error if optimizer state is missing
  - `--resume-model <ckpt>` is the intended path for the new default compact checkpoints such as `best.pth`
  - if strict continuation with optimizer / scheduler is needed later, save and use `last_resume.pth`
- Important runtime note:
  - the two bs8 pose runs on GPU `0` and GPU `3` were started before this save-policy change
  - therefore their finished artifacts still follow the old in-memory save path and include full-size `best.pth` and `last.pth`
- `pose_estimation/train_pose_estimation.py` now supports two distinct checkpoint continuation modes:
  - `--resume <ckpt>`:
    - full resume
    - restores model + optimizer + LR scheduler
    - resumes from the next epoch index saved in the checkpoint
  - `--resume-model <ckpt>`:
    - model-only resume / fine-tune
    - loads only model weights
    - uses a fresh optimizer + fresh LR scheduler from the current CLI hyperparameters
    - if the checkpoint contains an `epoch`, the run still starts from the next epoch index for logging / loop control
- Current pose checkpoints saved by `pose_estimation/train_pose_estimation.py` already contain enough information for both styles:
  - `model`
  - `optimizer`
  - `lr_scheduler`
  - `epoch`
  - `best_ap`
- `pose_estimation/engine.py` checkpoint loading is now more tolerant:
  - model-only loading accepts the repo's current pose checkpoints (`model`) as well as `alpred.py`-style checkpoints (`model_state_dict`)
- Important behavior fix:
  - resumed pose runs now inherit `best_ap` from the checkpoint instead of resetting it to `-1.0`
  - this avoids overwriting the previous best checkpoint incorrectly during later epochs

## 2026-04-09 Codex / OpenAI Account Switch Note

If you log out of OpenAI account 1 in VS Code Codex and then log in with account 2, do not assume the new account will seamlessly inherit the full chat context from account 1.

What persists:
- The local workspace state persists.
- Repository files, uncommitted edits, generated artifacts, and `git diff` remain in the same filesystem workspace.
- Local Codex state appears to keep session records separately from auth, so local resume may still be possible on the same machine.

What does not reliably persist across accounts:
- OpenAI-side chat history, memory, account-scoped workspace metadata, and other account-bound conversation context should be treated as separate between account 1 and account 2.
- The implicit context inside the current conversation should not be relied on after switching accounts.

Safe handoff rule:
- Write the current status, changed files, decisions, and next steps into `.handoff.md` before switching accounts.
- After switching accounts, continue from the codebase state plus this handoff file, not from the assumption that the prior chat thread is still fully available.
- If local session resume works, treat that as a convenience only, not as the primary handoff mechanism.

## Current State & Recent Accomplishments

### 1. Long-Tail Learning (LTL) Baselines Implementation
We actively developed and launched several LTL baselines in `utils/predict_ltl.py` to compare against our **Safefix** approach. We ran testing on **ImageNet** (GPU 1) and **CelebA** (GPU 2).
The newly implemented baselines include:
- **Focal Loss** (`--ltl_baseline focal`)
- **Class-Balanced Loss** (`--ltl_baseline class_weight`)
- **Oversampling** (`WeightedRandomSampler`)
- **LDAM Loss** (`--ltl_baseline ldam` based on Cao et al. NeurIPS 2019)

### 2. NeurIPS 2026 Paper Drafting
We completed the primary sections for the NeurIPS 2026 paper (`NeurIPS 2026 Attribute/neurips_2026.tex`). The drafts encapsulate:
- Methodologies comparing LTL baselines versus Safefix.
- Active Learning attribute analyses, rarity equations, and entropy mathematical foundations.
- Results transitioning from `report.md`.

### 3. Recent Computer Vision Experiments
Prior to the LTL baselines, we executed and summarized the training metrics (mAP, AP, AR) for:
- **YOLOv8** object detection model on the **KITTI** dataset.
- **RTMPose** on the **COCO** pose estimation dataset.
- Evaluated **AdaVision** dataset requirements.

2. If needed, compare held-out val vs held-out test slice summaries under `logs/pose/pose_keypointrcnn_train20k_*/slice_analysis/` to find the most stable failure slices.
